# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-29

Today adds 65 detailed guides and 50 additional screened discoveries, including completion of 6 earlier screened entries. The expanded search covers 73 fields, 599 configured repository queries in recent-activity, newly-created and established lanes, and 18 model tasks, supplemented by primary-source web research across every field. Highlights span agent-directed editing, speech and captions, 3D/CAD, music and neural DSP, motion, physical interaction and unusual computational art. New to this library does not mean newly launched. Every included entry has a complete software-license review, a concrete creative AI use and a dated six-check assessment. No app was installed or run. Independent reliability, model terms and hardware gaps remain explicit; no new entry earns Recommended or Ready to try from popularity or documentation alone. The existing library already had current reviews for the week of October 26; today’s newly published records receive fresh source assessments. Search limits, failed requests and source access gaps remain visible below. Bounded collection and source-review coverage are not proof of exhaustive discovery or hands-on quality approval. Repository attempts: 520 successful, 24 partial and 55 failed; 98 queries were bounded or incomplete. All 18 model tasks were attempted without a recorded model-query failure. Codeberg and SourceHut remained access gaps. These limits were supplemented with live primary-source research, not silently counted as successful repository searches.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Image-generation workspaces and local model adapters remain active. PhotoKit and SIQE receive guides; a browser UI does not establish GPU support or model permissions. [Source 1](https://open-aec.com/en/open-paint-studio) |
| Video, animation & film | Agent-directed editing and video generation appear across project sites and the repository search. Today’s guides distinguish editable timelines, cloud generation and conventional rendering. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) |
| Audio, music & voice | Neural synthesis, restoration and agent-controlled music tools were researched. The documented model choices have different licenses and hardware needs. [Source 1](https://ai.sony/blog/introducing-woosh-sony-ais-sound-effect-foundation-model) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | AI-assisted CAD and scene-authoring tools were researched alongside mesh generation. Export capability does not establish fabrication readiness. [Source 1](https://somacad.app/) |
| Browser tools & web media | AI-assisted web/design canvases were researched; local preview and project storage do not mean model inference is offline. [Source 1](https://openpencil.dev/) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | XRBlocks and related research broadened the XR search. A paper or interactive concept alone did not justify adding an unlicensed or unverified executable to the library. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24591) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Generative-art and neural-behaviour systems extend the search beyond image prompting. Today includes a neural artificial-life simulator and ICC-profile neural art. [Source 1](https://ossia.io/generative-art.html) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | Agent-controlled creative interfaces and real-time interaction were researched. Neural control is distinguished from ordinary event scripting. [Source 1](https://noisemaker.app/) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | AI-assisted CAD and layered artwork are represented; dimensional accuracy, material constraints and toolpath readiness still require separate validation. [Source 1](https://mitcad.org/) |
| Games & production pipelines | Generative worlds and agent-assisted game tooling were researched. Today’s engine guides distinguish learned simulation from an AI coding assistant. [Source 1](https://evoke-world.github.io/Evoke/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | Motion-generation and code-authored animation sources were researched. The report separates editable animation controls from claims of reliable autonomous motion design. [Source 1](https://anymotion.art/) |
| Avatars, digital humans & lip sync | Speech-driven and agent-controlled avatar workflows were researched. A purported real-time voice pipeline documents multi-minute rendering and external speech/model services. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16401) |
| VFX, compositing & relighting | AutoVFX and scene-editing research were checked as adjacent practices. No paper-only result is treated as a newly verified production plugin. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02394) |
| Spatial audio & volumetric media | Spatial reconstruction and interactive scene tools were researched beyond ordinary image/video editors. Headset support and spatial fidelity remain workflow-specific. [Source 1](https://proximap.space/) |
| Photogrammetry, scanning & neural rendering | Capture automation and neural perception were researched together. The AI-directed demo tools need explicit privacy and output review before recordings are shared. [Source 1](https://frontstage.studio/) |
| Editing, captions & post-production | Local transcription editors and agent-driven timelines were researched. Automatic captions, repeated-take removal and render checks do not remove the need to review the edit. [Source 1](https://openreelio.com/) |
| Vector graphics, illustration & textures | Vector drawing and agent-authored diagram sources were researched. DrawLab is retained as a screened lead because the current overview lacks a complete setup guide. [Source 1](https://kalka.design/) |
| Typography, fonts & layout | Neural variable-font and kinetic-type research was checked. No newly qualified standalone font tool was established solely from research titles. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05261) |
| Storyboarding, narrative & comics | Storyboard, comic and narrative workflows were researched. The manga guide explicitly separates permissive app code from a research/personal-use analysis model. [Source 1](https://zenstory.ai/) |
| Creative publishing & presentation | Slide/document and creator-production systems were researched. Agent publishing integrations are not authorization to publish user content. [Source 1](https://openslides.dev/) |
| Photography, restoration & color | Restoration and subject-aware framing were researched. Model-backed crop tools still require composition review and separate distribution checks. [Source 1](https://deoldify.ai/) |
| Data art & scientific visualization | Scientific/diagram visualization and live graphic-recording tools were researched. Diagram-agent syntax and semantic correctness are separate evidence checks. [Source 1](https://fluxsci.org/) |
| Physical, robotic & kinetic installations | Reactive and interactive installation practices were researched. Today’s lamp and wallpaper workflows do not establish unattended exhibition reliability. [Source 1](https://ghostarcade.live/docs/getting-started) |
| Performance, projection & stage media | Live audiovisual agents and music controls were researched. Optional proprietary DAWs, voice models and network services are disclosed separately. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/daydreamlive/scope) |
| Fashion, textiles & wearable media | Virtual try-on and apparel-design sources were checked. A new complete permissively licensed fashion workflow was not established from the supplemental web results. [Source 1](https://tryonlabs.github.io/opentryon/) |
| Accessible media & assistive creation | Tactile/vision and accessible-media workflows were researched. Useful accessibility intent does not establish reliable assistive performance or broad device support. [Source 1](https://dotifylabs.com/) |
| Mobile, edge & on-device creation | Mobile creative workflows were researched. DeepDenoiser Max has a documented Android route; desktop/browser projects are not relabeled as native mobile apps. [Source 1](https://shazacin.com/pages/open-source) |
| Creative learning & authoring | Visual explanation and interactive-learning tools were researched. Generated teaching material still needs factual and visual review. [Source 1](https://concat.fun/) |
| Archives, media restoration & collections | Cultural-media analysis and restoration sources were checked. Archival preservation is not inferred from an attractive restored preview. [Source 1](https://clams.ai/home/) |
| Emerging & cross-disciplinary creative AI | Adjacent practices include agent-operated physical interfaces, neural file-format artwork and emerging world models. Source eligibility is assessed separately from novelty. [Source 1](https://kyudan1.github.io/MuDuSi/) |
| Tactile, vibration and haptic media | HapticGen and tactile-fabrication research supplemented repository discovery. TactileLens receives a full guide with hardware/support gaps made explicit. [Source 1](https://hapticgen.hcitech.org/) |
| Neural acoustics and responsive sound spaces | SoundSpaces and room/audio research were examined. Ordinary acoustic simulation is not automatically classified as an AI tool. [Source 1](https://soundspaces.org/) |
| AI agents for code-authored media production | Creative-agent workspaces and control surfaces were researched across media. Rendering/control software is described as AI-assisted only where a concrete agent workflow is documented. [Source 1](https://nodetool.ai/) |
| AI kinetic typography and animated lettering | Kinetic-type diffusion and code-motion sources were checked. Authoring examples do not establish high-quality typography across arbitrary scripts or layouts. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11614) |
| AI choreography and dance composition | Music-conditioned dance and interactive choreography research were checked. No newly screened executable with complete licensing was established by the web supplement alone. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06722) |
| AI-assisted textile and computational craft | AI embroidery/craft research was checked, including a proposed atelier system. Conventional embroidery generators and unreleased research remain outside new eligible-tool counts. [Source 1](https://computationalcreativity.net/iccc24/papers/ICCC24_paper_167.pdf) |
| AI music notation and score recovery | Neural audio-to-score and music-notation sources were examined. Existing notation software is not AI merely because it accepts MIDI. [Source 1](https://muscriptor.kyutai.org/) |
| Neural relighting and material recovery | NVIDIA’s UniRelight model page supplemented the search. Model-card availability alone does not settle software licensing, local setup or professional lighting fidelity. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/UniRelight) |
| Olfactory and multisensory AI media | The Anemoia device research was checked as an adjacent sensory practice. No newly qualified installable open-source AI olfactory tool was established. [Source 1](https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/anemoia-device/overview/) |
| AI architectural visualization and spatial design | AI-assisted CAD, layout and plan conversion were researched. The kitchen-CAD lead is explicitly limited to its implemented prototype scope. [Source 1](https://docs.solidcraft.io/introduction.html) |
| Neural holography and light-field media | Neural light-field rendering and local radiance-field editing research were checked. No new operator-ready light-field tool was established solely from these papers. [Source 1](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2022/papers/Suhail_Light_Field_Neural_Rendering_CVPR_2022_paper.pdf) |
| Neural rigging and digital puppetry | Physical/robotic expressive interfaces were researched alongside avatar control. Mira Light is scoped to its documented vision and lamp-motion workflow. [Source 1](https://kyudan1.github.io/MuDuSi/) |
| AI ceramics and archaeological illustration | PyPottery and ClayScape research were examined. Research on ceramic processing or clay printing is not evidence of an independently usable, licensed fabrication package. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02072) |
| AI parametric design and jewelry | Agent-assisted parametric systems were checked. Formwright’s local model tiers and exported geometry need workflow-specific validation. [Source 1](https://tweakcad.com/open-source) |
| AI cartography and artistic maps | Mundi GIS documentation supplemented repository queries. Conventional thematic mapping projects were not included merely for being open source. [Source 1](https://docs.mundi.ai/) |
| AI robotic painting and drawing | Drawing-machine and robotics sources were researched. An open mechanical platform without a concrete implemented AI contribution does not qualify by itself. [Source 1](https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/open-drawing-machine/overview/) |
| AI cel animation and inbetweening | Animation and comic pipelines were examined. Manga narration and selective animation are distinguished from a complete automatic cel-animation system. [Source 1](https://coanimator.com/docs) |
| AI Foley and sound effects | Text/video-to-sound generation was researched. The Jetson Woosh interface is documented, while model permissions and broader device support remain separate checks. [Source 1](https://docs.ltx.io/open-source-model/feature-guides/audio/video-to-audio-foley) |
| Learned media codecs and neural delivery | Neural audio/video codec research was checked. A paper’s compression result is not sufficient to recommend an archival or production codec. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16608) |
| AI assisted sound data art and corpus instruments | Data-sonification and neural-music sources were researched. Conventional data-to-note mapping alone was not counted as AI. [Source 1](https://twotone.io/) |
| AI biosignal art and neurointeractive media | Timeflux and biosignal-related sources supplemented the EEG-to-music backlog review. Existing sparse guides remain pending rather than being reprinted unchanged. [Source 1](https://timeflux.io/) |
| Neural procedural material authoring | Neural material generation and conventional material-authoring sources were compared. Ordinary procedural nodes were not counted as neural generation. [Source 1](https://www.materialmaker.org/?mm_port=8000) |
| Neural cameras and unconventional image capture | Neural camera research and AI-camera documentation were examined. Camera hardware support does not establish a usable generative photography workflow. [Source 1](https://www.raspberrypi.com/documentation/accessories/ai-camera.html) |
| AI origami, kirigami and paper engineering | HarmonyCut research and layered-art tooling were researched. SVG layers are a design starting point, not proof of a structurally sound cut assembly. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07628) |
| AI landscape, garden and environmental design | The broader design-canvas and CAD search did not establish a newly eligible AI landscape-design application with reviewed software terms. This remains an explicit gap. [Source 1](https://doop.design/) |
| Neural mosaic and stained-glass media | Pixel/mosaic generation services and package results were checked. A hosted model endpoint is not proof of an open-source, locally reproducible application. [Source 1](https://replicate.com/smkrv/entropixel) |
| AI optical illusions and anamorphic media | Diffusion illusions and neural colour-profile artwork were examined. iccnet documents specific engine differences rather than universal image-viewer support. [Source 1](https://diffusionillusions.com/assets/DiffusionIllusions_WithAppendix_2024.pdf) |
| AI relief engraving and learned halftoning | Plotter/laser preparation sources were checked. Conventional tracing and plotting tools were not promoted into the AI library without a concrete AI workflow. [Source 1](https://www.plotcaster.com/) |
| Neural brushstroke painting and drawn media | Stroke-based drawing and agent-generated vector work were researched. DrawLab’s source is present, but setup and creative-result evidence remain incomplete. [Source 1](https://www.aria-art.com/) |
| AI color direction and palette-based media | Colour-look and perception sources were checked. Source-available commercial colour software was not treated as verified open-source software. [Source 1](https://hance.video/) |
| AI collage, cut-paper composition and layered media | Design canvases and multi-source creative composition were searched. No additional standalone collage application passed today’s complete eligibility review. [Source 1](https://metastable.studio/) |
| AI stereo conversion and spatial video authoring | Stereo conversion and depth-animation sources were checked. Browser playback and stereo export do not prove headset-specific comfort or fidelity. [Source 1](https://elastic3d.github.io/) |
| AI semantic editing of captured 3D scenes | Language-guided splat/head editing research was examined. A recent paper remains a research finding until executable source, license and workflow are reviewed. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03599) |
| AI signing avatars and expressive gesture media | Signing-avatar projects and language-specific translation systems were researched. Animation alone does not establish linguistically accurate sign communication. [Source 1](https://vh.cmp.uea.ac.uk/index.php/CWA_Signing_Avatars) |
| AI assisted audio plugin and DSP authoring | Neural plugin/audio SDK work and agent-directed sound tools were checked. Code generation for DSP and running a trained audio network are separate workflows. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09126) |
| AI panoramic worlds and virtual tour creation | Interactive world-generation sources were examined. A steerable demo does not by itself establish persistent world consistency or VR support. [Source 1](https://evoke-world.github.io/Evoke/) |
| Learned garment simulation and digital clothing | DiT-Garment and clothing research were checked. No newly qualified garment-production package was established by the supplemental research alone. [Source 1](https://dumoulina.github.io/dit-garment/) |
| AI visual dubbing and translated performance | Dubbing and lip-sync systems were researched. OpenDub’s project site explicitly separates speech dubbing from lip-sync and model/service terms remain distinct. [Source 1](https://opendub.app/) |
| Neural video reshooting and camera control | Reshoot-Anything’s model documentation was checked. Camera-control research and a model card do not establish independently reproducible local editing. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/morphic/reshoot-anything/blob/main/README.md?code=true) |
| Neural denoising for real-time creative rendering | Neural render-denoising documentation supplemented the engine search. OHAO’s guide is scoped to its documented denoising integration, not every planned engine feature. [Source 1](https://pmndrs.github.io/denoiser/docs/) |
| AI assisted media quality and take selection | Media evaluation and learned plausibility tools were checked. Scores are not a substitute for workflow-specific creative assessment. [Source 1](https://evalmedia.com/) |
| AI print separation and prepress authoring | Quality/halftone research was examined alongside the retained Deep-Halftoning lead. Its unresolved reproduction/setup gaps do not justify an unchanged repeat or print-readiness claim. [Source 1](https://vqeg.org/projects/vime/) |
| Interactive neural game worlds | Steerable world models, neural simulation and agent-native engines were searched. The report distinguishes small learned-simulation experiments from general game engines. [Source 1](https://matrix-game-v2.github.io/) |

## Search and review counts

73 creative fields; 599/599 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 22747 distinct source candidates and 856 model leads. 65 detailed profiles and 50 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 55 failed repository queries, 24 partial repository queries, 98 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: OpenDub’s GitLab project and official site were examined. The collector’s GitLab API request was refused by its host allowlist, while web access reached project metadata. A complete software-license snapshot was not obtained, so this did not become a new screened tool. [Source](https://gitlab.com/open-dubapp/OpenDub) [Source](https://opendub.app/)
- **codeberg** · gap: Live web access was blocked by robots restrictions. No source result or license could be verified from this ecosystem in this run.
- **sourcehut** · gap: Live web access was blocked by robots restrictions. This is a coverage gap, not evidence that the ecosystem has no relevant tools.
- **packages** · searched: CaptionForge’s PyPI documentation describes local faster-whisper captions and optional Argos translation; package availability alone was not accepted as complete source/license review. [Source](https://pypi.org/project/captionforge/)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: The Comfy H3 Latent Relay registry entry and primary source were researched. Its guide requires ComfyUI 0.35.0 or newer with H3 support; loading nodes on older hosts does not prove the workflow works. [Source](https://registry.comfy.org/nodes/h3-latent-relay)
- **project-sites** · searched: Creative project sites across video, CAD, interactive media, sound and emerging practices supplemented every observed field. Site claims were treated as discovery evidence, not automatic eligibility or quality approval. [Source](https://openfilm.dev/) [Source](https://somacad.app/) [Source](https://hapticgen.hcitech.org/)
- **research-code** · searched: Research project/model documentation covered relighting, neural camera control, haptics, sound and world models. Paper-only or model-only evidence was not promoted into a fully reviewed application. [Source](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/UniRelight) [Source](https://huggingface.co/morphic/reshoot-anything/blob/main/README.md?code=true) [Source](https://evoke-world.github.io/Evoke/)
- **international** · searched: Chinese, French, Spanish, Japanese and Ukrainian-language workflows were examined. Examples include H3 relay, manga-anim, SmartCrop, Japanese voice synthesis and Holos; platform and language limits are explicit. [Source](https://registry.comfy.org/nodes/h3-latent-relay) [Source](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim) [Source](https://github.com/nlink-jp/voice-studio-mcp) [Source](https://github.com/BrainFromUkraine/holos-studio)

## OpenKatana

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · AI kinetic typography and animated lettering · AI agents for code-authored media production

New to the full-guide library; the repository was created 2026-10-16. Repository creation is not proof of a stable release or a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Authoring repeatable promos, explainers and motion typography while keeping editable project files and local rendering.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-29. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official README links Koji and MOTIF films plus editable examples. MOTIF demonstrates agent-authored procedural Blender production, not a video-generation model benchmark. Published examples were documented, not independently reproduced. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Python 3.11+, Git, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome/Chromium. Clone the source and use its setup script to create an environment and first project. Blender and model runtimes are optional separate installs. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The recent release snapshot includes v0.1.1 (2026-10-29T05:15:34Z, published release). No non-pull-request issue was present in the bounded recent-activity response; this is not a claim that no defects exist. Up to five releases and ten recently updated issue/PR records were inspected. This does not establish sustained support or resolve workflow-specific blockers. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/openkatana/releases) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/openkatana/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** The documentation, examples and bounded issue activity were examined for this workflow. They did not establish reliable independent first-hand use or an independently working integration. Publisher tests and screenshots alone are insufficient. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/openkatana/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Bundled fonts retain OFL terms; YuNet, Three.js and cannon-es retain MIT notices. Optional RIFE, Real-ESRGAN, ComfyUI and generation weights keep their own licenses. LTX sample generation used separately licensed model/service access. Local rendering plus optional agent/provider/model costs; no included general-purpose AI account. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Loopback, single-user editing. Advanced composition needs agent/CLI authoring. Tracking, identity consistency, HDR effects and arbitrary 3D assets need workflow-specific checks; paid-provider integration is not broadly certified.

### How it uses AI

An agent-driven editing engine turns a brief, existing footage, photos and music into a rendered timeline. CLI, MCP, Studio and ComfyUI interfaces expose the editing operations; optional models add generation and vision. [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

An agent-driven editing engine turns a brief, existing footage, photos and music into a rendered timeline. CLI, MCP, Studio and ComfyUI interfaces expose the editing operations; optional models add generation and vision. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Authoring repeatable promos, explainers and motion typography while keeping editable project files and local rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The official README links Koji and MOTIF films plus editable examples. MOTIF demonstrates agent-authored procedural Blender production, not a video-generation model benchmark. Published examples were documented, not independently reproduced. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Install

Install Python 3.11+, Git, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome/Chromium. Clone the source and use its setup script to create an environment and first project. Blender and model runtimes are optional separate installs. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Python 3.11+, Git, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome/Chromium. Clone the source and use its setup script to create an environment and first project. Blender and model runtimes are optional separate installs.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana.git
```


```sh
cd OpenKatana
```


```sh
python scripts/setup.py
```


```sh
openkatana doctor
```


```sh
openkatana studio projects/my-first-film
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Open the prepared project in Studio and import your own footage and music.
2. Give a compatible agent a brief and inspect its proposed timeline and frames.
3. Render a short preview, revise timing and typography, then export.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** A dedicated GPU is not required for ordinary editing/rendering. Optional model and Blender workloads have separate requirements; minimum RAM, VRAM and storage are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+, Git, FFmpeg/ffprobe, Chrome/Chromium; optional Blender, ComfyUI and an independently configured agent.
- **Platforms:** Windows is locally tested by the maintainer; Windows/Linux CI is described. macOS setup commands exist but macOS is explicitly untested. Browser access does not establish platform certification.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Bundled fonts retain OFL terms; YuNet, Three.js and cannon-es retain MIT notices. Optional RIFE, Real-ESRGAN, ComfyUI and generation weights keep their own licenses. LTX sample generation used separately licensed model/service access.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Bundled fonts retain OFL terms; YuNet, Three.js and cannon-es retain MIT notices. Optional RIFE, Real-ESRGAN, ComfyUI and generation weights keep their own licenses. LTX sample generation used separately licensed model/service access.
- **Cost:** Local rendering plus optional agent/provider/model costs; no included general-purpose AI account.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Limitations

Loopback, single-user editing. Advanced composition needs agent/CLI authoring. Tracking, identity consistency, HDR effects and arbitrary 3D assets need workflow-specific checks; paid-provider integration is not broadly certified. [Source 1](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/docs/CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/rk0neki/OpenKatana/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Get the tool

## Aftertake

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · VFX, compositing & relighting · AI agents for code-authored media production

New to the full-guide library; the repository was created 2026-10-17. Repository creation is not proof of a stable release or a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Developing a short film from generated shots, then controlling cuts, color, overlays and sound in one project.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-29. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current README explicitly describes Aftertake as an independent experimental project; native generation and model-specific memory behavior remain constrained by the documented hardware validation scope. https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Maintainer examples include a 93-second film with 72 shots and downloadable project material. The recorded editor/examples need no GPU; this review did not run them or validate generative output quality. [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node.js 22+ and Git, clone the repository, and launch the examples first. The separate generation installer targets Windows/Linux x86-64, Python 3.12 and a compatible NVIDIA driver; use its hardware checks before obtaining weights. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The recent release snapshot includes then-comes-the-aftertake-2026-10-22 (2026-10-22T13:02:26Z, published release). No non-pull-request issue was present in the bounded recent-activity response; this is not a claim that no defects exist. Up to five releases and ten recently updated issue/PR records were inspected. This does not establish sustained support or resolve workflow-specific blockers. ### How it uses AI

A local film editor connects ComfyUI generation, character replacement, SAM3 tracking, titles, audio and an editable multitrack timeline. [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

### Introduction

A local film editor connects ComfyUI generation, character replacement, SAM3 tracking, titles, audio and an editable multitrack timeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

### What it is good for

Developing a short film from generated shots, then controlling cuts, color, overlays and sound in one project. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

### Demo & examples

Maintainer examples include a 93-second film with 72 shots and downloadable project material. The recorded editor/examples need no GPU; this review did not run them or validate generative output quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

### Install

Install Node.js 22+ and Git, clone the repository, and launch the examples first. The separate generation installer targets Windows/Linux x86-64, Python 3.12 and a compatible NVIDIA driver; use its hardware checks before obtaining weights. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node.js 22+ and Git, clone the repository, and launch the examples first. The separate generation installer targets Windows/Linux x86-64, Python 3.12 and a compatible NVIDIA driver; use its hardware checks before obtaining weights.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake.git
```


```sh
cd aftertake
```


```sh
node scripts/aftertake.mjs --examples
```


```sh
node scripts/aftertake.mjs setup-gpu --video
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ItsSNOWMOBILE/aftertake/blob/aftertake-studio/docs/hardware.md)

- **Hardware:** The documented development host is RTX 5080 with 16 GB VRAM and 32 GB RAM. These are tested-host facts, not universal minima. No RTX 3090/12 GB end-to-end certification or total disk minimum is claimed.
- **Software:** Node.js 22+; generation uses pinned ComfyUI/CUDA/Python 3.12 components. Character work additionally needs FFmpeg/ffprobe. CPU film editing has a separate runtime.
- **Platforms:** Recorded examples/editor are available without NVIDIA. Local generation targets Windows/Linux x86-64; Linux CPU CI does not certify Linux GPU inference. No native Apple Silicon generation certification is claimed.

Scope: Generating and assembling short scenes on a high-memory Apple Silicon Mac, with a timeline that combines video, overlays and audio.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-29. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/OpenMagia/blob/main/LICENSE)

Existing models can be configured instead of downloaded again. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Maintenance and support · unknown:** No release was returned in the recent-release snapshot. The bounded recent-activity review includes #1 closed: Failed to generate. Up to five releases and ten recently updated issue/PR records were inspected. This does not establish sustained support or resolve workflow-specific blockers. [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/openmagia/releases) [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/openmagia/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** The documentation, examples and bounded issue activity were examined for this workflow. They did not establish reliable independent first-hand use or an independently working integration. Publisher tests and screenshots alone are insufficient. [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/OpenMagia/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/openmagia/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** OpenMagia code is AGPL-3.0-only. H3 weights use the MiniMax community license. Optional Qwen has Apache terms; YuE2 runtime is Apache but its documented weights are CC BY-NC 4.0, restricting commercial use. Local compute/storage; optional model access and model-specific commercial restrictions remain separate. [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/OpenMagia/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/OpenMagia/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/davidaircloud/OpenMagia/blob/main/docs/YUE_MUSIC_INTEGRATION.md)

Optional music has no hard duration/BPM/reference-audio control and no complete score-editing history in the UI. The release PR document is a historical design record, not current release proof.

### How it uses AI

A local browser-based nonlinear editor uses MiniMax-H3 through h3.c for scene generation, reference characters and shot chaining, with optional Qwen prompt refinement and YuE2 music. - **Hardware:** Native H3 documentation specifies Apple Silicon with 64 GB unified memory and roughly 278 GB for FL2VA/Ref2VA weights. Optional YuE2 lists 7.3 GB model plus 0.53 GB VAE; 32 GB MPS measurements are examples, not a universal minimum.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, make/build tools, FFmpeg/ffprobe and h3.c; optional llama.cpp/Qwen and a separate YuE2 runtime.

### How it uses AI

A Go/libav graph engine embeds optional whisper.cpp speech recognition into media pipelines, producing timestamped subtitle and transcript sidecars while retaining the audio stream. - **Commercial:** Software use follows LGPL-2.1. Project code uses LGPL-2.1-or-later. Linked FFmpeg options can change combined binary obligations to GPL or make nonfree builds nonredistributable. Models are not bundled; Whisper and optional YOLO/SFace/Vidi models retain their own terms.
- **Cost:** Local transcription needs compute and model storage. Optional TwelveLabs and other hosted integrations have separate access and charges.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Camera mismatch, occlusion, source-video shadows and UV seams limit the result. Steps 4–9 are a research snapshot tied to absent intermediate data; a texture export is not a printable-mesh guarantee.

### How it uses AI

A known Blender camera orbit is restyled by an AI video model, then a differentiable rasterizer fits those video frames back into a UV texture on the original mesh. [Source](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A known Blender camera orbit is restyled by an AI video model, then a differentiable rasterizer fits those video frames back into a UV texture on the original mesh. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

### What it is good for

Exploring stylized surface treatments while retaining existing geometry and camera calibration. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

### Demo & examples

A 42-second explainer and four-view car renders document the fitted atlas and cleanup comparisons. The reported 4K/8K metrics concern one recorded asset and were not reproduced here. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

### Install

Obtain the source, Blender, Python 3.10+, FFmpeg, a fal account/API key and fal CLI. Prepare your own scene and follow steps 1–3; the fitting route is deployed to a cloud H100. Later steps reference intermediate data that is not shipped. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the source, Blender, Python 3.10+, FFmpeg, a fal account/API key and fal CLI. Prepare your own scene and follow steps 1–3; the fitting route is deployed to a cloud H100. Later steps reference intermediate data that is not shipped.

```sh
blender -b scene.blend -P pipeline/01_orbit/rig_v2.py
```


```sh
python pipeline/02_ai_video/generate.py reference --frame clay_frames/frame_0001.png
```


```sh
python pipeline/03_fit/prepare_dataset.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

1. Render a calibrated clay orbit of a mesh you can use.
2. Generate a style reference and video, inspect alignment, then prepare masks/dataset for fitting.
3. Fit the UV atlas and inspect unseen surfaces/seams; adapt later completion stages only after supplying their missing inputs.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The documented fitting service uses an H100. Local RAM/VRAM/disk minima and an alternative local GPU route are not established.
- **Software:** Blender headless, Python 3.10+, FFmpeg, fal CLI/account; documented cloud fitting uses nvdiffrast and a pinned CUDA/PyTorch environment.
- **Platforms:** Local Blender plus cloud inference is documented; no complete local Windows/macOS/Linux inference matrix is established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/lovisdotio/video-to-texture/blob/master/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers project code. Seedream, Seedance, Topaz and PATINA services, nvdiffrast, UVgami and the source 3D asset have separate terms. The example car asset is not included.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers project code. Seedream, Seedance, Topaz and PATINA services, nvdiffrast, UVgami and the source 3D asset have separate terms. The example car asset is not included.
- **Cost:** Paid fal generation, cloud fitting and optional cleanup/upscale calls; recorded historical costs are not current quotes.

### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Base and VoiceDesign are about 2.9 GB each, 5.8 GB together. The README reports 6.97 GB peak memory in one test, not a minimum RAM guarantee; VRAM and total install space are not specified.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, Apple MLX and the documented Qwen3-TTS 1.7B 8-bit model variants.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon macOS only for the current local TTS backend; the previous PyTorch fallback has been removed. Windows/Linux are not supported by this route.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Software is Apache-2.0. Qwen/MLX model variants retain their model-card terms; optional Suno music uses an external service and is not covered by the local TTS privacy claim.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Software is Apache-2.0. Qwen/MLX model variants retain their model-card terms; optional Suno music uses an external service and is not covered by the local TTS privacy claim.
- **Cost:** Local TTS uses local compute after downloads. Optional online music has provider-specific costs and network behavior.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Cloning does not honor dynamic tone/emotion instructions in the current MLX Base path. Missing reference text produces poor/truncated output and is rejected. Distribution is a separate project, not part of this tool. [Source 1](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/webkubor/voxflow/blob/main/README.md)

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/README.md)

- **Software:** Python 3.10+, FFmpeg/ffprobe, Parakeet model download; optional faster-whisper and a separately configured MCP assistant.
- **Platforms:** FFmpeg setup is documented for Windows, macOS and Linux. The default transcription route is CPU/English; GPU and non-English paths require the optional Whisper setup.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app code. Parakeet and Whisper weights retain separate terms; optional online source access and the assistant’s model provider are separate dependencies.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT app code. Parakeet and Whisper weights retain separate terms; optional online source access and the assistant’s model provider are separate dependencies.
- **Cost:** Local transcription needs no cloud transcription key; a chosen hosted assistant may still incur costs.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Frames are sampled, not continuously understood. Speech recognition may mishear names and the CPU multilingual route can be slow. Platform subtitles may replace model transcription when available. [Source 1](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/marwanm7moud/watch-video/blob/main/README.md)

```sh
git clone https://github.com/ToBeWin/make-motion-comic.git
```


```sh
cd make-motion-comic
```


```sh
python3 -m pip install edge-tts
```


```sh

```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ToBeWin/make-motion-comic/blob/main/README.md)

1. Prepare script, original character reference sheets and shot-by-shot keyframes.
2. Create separate dialogue recordings and derive actual timings/subtitles from their durations.
3. Render individual pan/zoom/hold shots, assemble and manually inspect character drift, jitter, subtitles and the final audio mix.

### How it uses AI

A self-hosted visual canvas connects text, reference images, video and hosted AI models into queued, reusable generation/editing graphs. [Source](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A self-hosted visual canvas connects text, reference images, video and hosted AI models into queued, reusable generation/editing graphs. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

### What it is good for

Building image-to-video and multimodal workflows with shared templates, asset storage and per-run cost estimates. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official live demo and editor screenshots are linked. The project reports live Gaorui tests, while OpenAI/Gemini and non-MinIO S3 backends remain untested by the project. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

### Install

Install Docker Compose v2, clone the repository, copy its environment template and configure a private encryption key/admin password. Start the stack, sign in locally and add a provider using your own credentials. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Docker Compose v2, clone the repository, copy its environment template and configure a private encryption key/admin password. Start the stack, sign in locally and add a provider using your own credentials.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas.git loomcanvas
```


```sh
cd loomcanvas
```


```sh
cp .env.example .env
```


```sh
docker compose up -d
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

1. Add a provider and a price table appropriate to your account.
2. Connect a prompt/reference node to one generation node and inspect a small result.
3. Save a template or run a batch, then inspect actual provider billing separately from estimates.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/gaoruitech/genrelay-loomcanvas/blob/master/README.md)

- **Hardware:** No local inference GPU is required. Server RAM, CPU and storage minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Docker Compose v2; source development needs Node.js 20.9+ and MongoDB 7. Browser frontend and provider API access.


Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Animation, some serialization and older integrations have been removed. Differentiable and inverse rendering are not promised by this repository.

### How it uses AI

A Vulkan renderer with Intel Open Image Denoise for offline neural denoising and a separate NVIDIA DLSS reconstruction path. [Source](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A Vulkan renderer with Intel Open Image Denoise for offline neural denoising and a separate NVIDIA DLSS reconstruction path. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

### What it is good for

Rendering GLB assets and turntables while comparing noisy and denoised images. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README provides rendered examples and an author-measured real-time helmet scene; that real-time result does not validate the offline workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

### Install

Install the documented Vulkan and C++ build dependencies, configure CMake, build, and follow the model_viewer GLB rendering example. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the documented Vulkan and C++ build dependencies, configure CMake, build, and follow the model_viewer GLB rendering example.

```sh
cmake -B build -S . -DFETCHCONTENT_UPDATES_DISCONNECTED=ON
```


```sh
cmake --build build -j8
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

1. Load a small GLB in the documented viewer.
2. Render a reference image and apply the supported denoiser.
3. Inspect texture detail and temporal consistency before rendering a turntable.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

- **Hardware:** A compatible Vulkan ray-tracing GPU is required for the documented rendering path. Minimum system RAM and VRAM are not specified.
- **Software:** C++20 compiler, CMake 3.20+, Vulkan SDK 1.3; backend-specific denoising dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Linux GCC/Clang and Windows MSVC are documented. macOS is not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Qervas/ohao_engine/blob/master/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT engine code; OIDN, NVIDIA DLSS and asset terms are separate. DLSS requires compatible NVIDIA hardware and its own SDK terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT engine code; OIDN, NVIDIA DLSS and asset terms are separate. DLSS requires compatible NVIDIA hardware and its own SDK terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

An agent-directed motion-design toolkit combines structured briefs, reusable styles and executable rendering workflows for code-authored graphics. [Source](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An agent-directed motion-design toolkit combines structured briefs, reusable styles and executable rendering workflows for code-authored graphics. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Creating short brand animations and matching horizontal and vertical deliveries. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The project includes style demonstrations and an author-reported Apple Silicon render example; these are not independent output reviews. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the Claude Code marketplace package or use the documented compatible-agent route, then prepare the listed Node, Python and rendering dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the Claude Code marketplace package or use the documented compatible-agent route, then prepare the listed Node, Python and rendering dependencies.

```sh
/plugin marketplace add ANVEAI/motion-pack
```


```sh
/plugin install motion-pack@anve
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

1. Write a brief with dimensions and duration.
2. Create a small project in one documented style.
3. Render, inspect frames and typography, then adapt the alternate aspect ratio.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node.js 22+, Python 3.11 virtual environment and FFmpeg; individual Blender/Three.js/HyperFrames routes add their own dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon examples are documented. Confirm the selected rendering backend on other platforms; universal support is not inferred.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ANVEAI/motion-pack/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. An old host may load nodes while failing to provide the expected capability. Finite-run concatenation can retain all media in RAM; start with two segments. Default seam processing changes audio.

### How it uses AI

ComfyUI nodes carry video and audio latent tails between MiniMax H3 segments, supporting continued clips without repeatedly decoding and re-encoding the context. [Source](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

ComfyUI nodes carry video and audio latent tails between MiniMax H3 segments, supporting continued clips without repeatedly decoding and re-encoding the context. [Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Constructing longer audiovisual sequences from short model generations with explicit continuity controls. [Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

[Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Install

Install the custom nodes into an H3-capable ComfyUI, restart, load the supplied minimal workflow and configure all four required model components. [Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the custom nodes into an H3-capable ComfyUI, restart, load the supplied minimal workflow and configure all four required model components.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Run the first segment and keep its run ID.
2. Generate the next segment using the saved context slots.
3. Take audio through the documented trim path and inspect joins before concatenating a longer sequence.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ZenHG/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zenhg/ComfyUI-H3-Latent-Relay/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** H3 model-dependent GPU/RAM requirements apply; the node documentation does not supply one universal VRAM minimum. Longer in-memory concatenation increases RAM use.
- **Software:** ComfyUI 0.35.0 or newer with the required H3 support, Python 3.10+, torch and safetensors; PyAV 17+ for concatenation. Verify the exact ComfyUI version stated by the project before setup.
- **Platforms:** ComfyUI host platforms apply only where the H3 backend works; platform-specific model acceleration was not independently verified.

### Introduction

A badminton editing workstation combines manual timelines with optional shuttle/pose/image models and agent-assisted review of rally clips. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing sports highlights while preserving uncertain boundaries for human review. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

### Demo & examples

The bundled example is an already edited 13-segment video, about 2 minutes 14 seconds. It tests playback/editing, not independent rally-recognition accuracy. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

### Install

On Windows, install Python and use the release ZIP with start.bat. Source builds additionally require Node 24+ and pnpm 11.25.0. Configure optional models separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On Windows, install Python and use the release ZIP with start.bat. Source builds additionally require Node 24+ and pnpm 11.25.0. Configure optional models separately.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

1. Import a match recording and choose an analysis configuration.
2. Review uncertain intervals and manually adjust cuts.
3. Export a new MP4 and compare it with the source footage.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+ (3.12 recommended); release includes built frontend. Source route adds Node.js 24+ and pnpm 11.25.0.
- **Platforms:** Windows x64 is the initial acceptance target; Linux/macOS are explicitly unverified.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 code; footage, third-party components, model weights and cloud image services have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code; footage, third-party components, model weights and cloud image services have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

### Limitations

Basic motion/sound analysis needs no GPU or key but does not prove AI recognition. Registered models are not necessarily accuracy-validated; the local service has no multiuser isolation. [Source 1](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skry1412/CourtCut/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/SKRY1412/CourtCut/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current 0.x project format can change, reliability hardening continues and AMD/Intel GPU validation is incomplete. VST2/VST3 are unsupported.

### How it uses AI

A native Linux timeline editor combines local transcription, vision, narration and speech enhancement with an MCP-controlled editing workflow. [Source](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

### Introduction

A native Linux timeline editor combines local transcription, vision, narration and speech enhancement with an MCP-controlled editing workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

### What it is good for

Editing and grading footage on Linux, including transcript cuts and reviewed agent changes. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

### Demo & examples

The project links an editor showcase and colour/title/transcript screenshots. Its own tests and reference comparisons are not independent user validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

### Install

Use the signed Arch package and verify its published signature/checksums, or build the packaging directory on Arch. Select the CPU ONNX runtime unless choosing a compatible GPU backend. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the signed Arch package and verify its published signature/checksums, or build the packaging directory on Arch. Select the CPU ONNX runtime unless choosing a compatible GPU backend.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp.git
```


```sh
cd klipp/packaging
```


```sh
makepkg -si
```


```sh
klipp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

1. Import a short clip and save a project copy.
2. Try transcription or one assistant edit.
3. Review the timeline, grade and export before committing a larger project.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

- **Hardware:** Vulkan 1.1-capable GPU required; integrated hardware support is provisional. RAM/VRAM and initial build disk-space minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Rust source workspace and Arch build dependencies; ONNX Runtime for models. A working Vulkan driver is required.
- **Platforms:** Primary target: x86_64 Arch Linux. Other Linux distributions are less tested; no macOS/Windows package is established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/davidkodar/klipp/blob/main/docs/src/ai-tools/models.md)

An external assistant may use paid cloud inference.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0 code; downloaded models, plugins and assets retain separate terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current v0.1 is explicitly experimental. The browser UI lacks word timestamps, phones are unmeasured and live inference repeatedly reads unfinished audio.

### How it uses AI

A Rust/WebAssembly implementation performs Parakeet speech recognition on the browser CPU for recordings and live microphone input. [Source](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### Introduction

A Rust/WebAssembly implementation performs Parakeet speech recognition on the browser CPU for recordings and live microphone input. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### What it is good for

Preparing transcripts of interviews, narration and media recordings without uploading the audio after model loading. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### Demo & examples

A hosted demo and reproducible author benchmarks are linked. The M4 Pro timing is hardware-specific and not an independent benchmark. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### Install

Try the hosted browser page, or clone recursively and run the documented just ui build, which downloads tools and the pinned checkpoint. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Try the hosted browser page, or clone recursively and run the documented just ui build, which downloads tools and the pinned checkpoint.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm
```


```sh
cd parakeet-redux-wasm
```


```sh
just ui
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

1. Load the model in Chrome or Edge.
2. Transcribe a short file or microphone sample.
3. Review language accuracy and provisional live words before using the transcript.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

- **Hardware:** About 199 MB model bundle from a 178 MB checkpoint. Threaded runtime memory may grow to 2 GB; a GPU is not required.
- **Software:** Source route: Rust 1.99+, Node.js 22+, just and WASM toolchain. Browser route requires a compatible modern browser.
- **Platforms:** Chrome/Edge are the documented browser test targets. Safari/Firefox fallback builds were not tested in those browsers; native CLI measurements use Apple Silicon macOS.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application code; Parakeet Redux weights are CC-BY-4.0 and require their own attribution. No hosted transcription API is required.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application code; Parakeet Redux weights are CC-BY-4.0 and require their own attribution. No hosted transcription API is required.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### Limitations

The current v0.1 is explicitly experimental. The browser UI lacks word timestamps, phones are unmeasured and live inference repeatedly reads unfinished audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/fusorlabs/parakeet-redux-wasm)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://github.com/fusor-rs/parakeet-redux-wasm/blob/main/README.md)
