### How it uses AI

Rust CLI with Python/MLX workers that detects manga panels, text and speakers with Magi v2, voices them with Kokoro and optionally animates panels or generates new ones. [Source](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Rust CLI with Python/MLX workers that detects manga panels, text and speakers with Magi v2, voices them with Kokoro and optionally animates panels or generates new ones. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Turning rights-cleared comic pages into a narrated edit with inspectable panels, cast assignments and an editable timeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents the import, analysis, reader, narration and MP4 workflow. Its examples are developer documentation, not an independently reproduced film. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

On Apple Silicon macOS install Rust, FFmpeg, uv and Poppler for PDF; build the CLI and synchronize the workers with the Japanese extra if needed. Keep the CLI beside its workers or follow the packaged-prefix layout. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On Apple Silicon macOS install Rust, FFmpeg, uv and Poppler for PDF; build the CLI and synchronize the workers with the Japanese extra if needed. Keep the CLI beside its workers or follow the packaged-prefix layout.

```sh
brew install ffmpeg uv poppler
```


```sh
cargo build --release
```


```sh
cd workers && uv sync --extra ja
```


```sh
manga doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import a folder of owned CBZ/PDF pages.
2. Create a project range, run analyze, and use read to correct OCR and speaker assignments.
3. Configure cast voices, run voice and timeline, inspect the timeline JSON, then render the MP4.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Magi downloads about 4 GB; optional Wan about 34 GB and FLUX about 15 GB downloaded/5 GB retained are documented. These are model storage figures, not RAM minima. Minimum RAM and VRAM are not documented.
- **Software:** Rust, Python workers managed by uv, FFmpeg; Poppler for PDFs, unar for CBR. Optional generation uses separate MLX environments to avoid incompatible Transformers versions.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon macOS is the documented target; Windows and Linux operation is not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/maxgfr/manga-anim/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** Magi v2 is explicitly research/personal-use only; Kokoro, Wan2.2 and FLUX.2 klein 4B are described separately as Apache-2.0. The MIT application grant does not remove the Magi restriction or comic copyrights.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Magi v2 is explicitly research/personal-use only; Kokoro, Wan2.2 and FLUX.2 klein 4B are described separately as Apache-2.0. The MIT application grant does not remove the Magi restriction or comic copyrights.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

- **Hardware:** NVIDIA 8 GB VRAM is recommended; 16 GB is recommended for ACE-Step, or Apple Silicon. CPU operation is slower. Engine installation is about 3–6 GB; model downloads range from about 2.4 to 11 GB in the listed options. Minimum RAM is not documented.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+, Electron; local engine uses uv or Python 3.10–3.12 with PyTorch. Model downloads and optional cloud credentials are separate.
- **Platforms:** Source packaging commands are documented for Windows, macOS and Linux; support claims are not local test results.

### Introduction

An Android fork of DeepDenoiser using DeepFilterNet3 through ONNX Runtime to suppress noise in recordings and imported audio/video. [Source 1](https://github.com/rajbhx/deepdenoiser-max/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Cleaning dialogue or location sound on an Android phone, with adjustable wet/dry strength and export. [Source 1](https://github.com/rajbhx/deepdenoiser-max/blob/main/README.md)

Use the project source build with Bun, Java 17, Android SDK 36 and NDK 28.1. Run Expo prebuild and Gradle. Development signing is not a production release identity. [Source 1](https://github.com/rajbhx/deepdenoiser-max/blob/main/README.md)

```sh
bun install
```


```sh
bunx expo prebuild --platform android --clean --no-install
```


```sh
cd android && ./gradlew :app:assembleRelease
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/rajbhx/deepdenoiser-max/blob/main/README.md)

2. Apply neural denoising and compare at matched levels with the original.

### Introduction

A Rust/WASM artificial-life simulation whose creatures inherit and mutate neural-network brains, rendered as an interactive WebGL2 ecology. [Source 1](https://github.com/brunoqgalvao/rep/blob/main/README.md)

Exploring emergent movement, lineage colour and ecological dynamics for generative-art studies or interactive teaching. [Source 1](https://github.com/brunoqgalvao/rep/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Rust 1.90+, Node 18+ and wasm-pack, add the WASM target, then start the web workspace. [Source 1](https://github.com/brunoqgalvao/rep/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Rust 1.90+, Node 18+ and wasm-pack, add the WASM target, then start the web workspace.

```sh
rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown
```


```sh
cd web && npm i && npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/brunoqgalvao/rep/blob/main/README.md)

2. Adjust food and time speed; inspect a creature’s genes and brain.
3. Compare population/lineage behaviour across controlled runs.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Ordinary browsers show the gradient. Table-based pipelines produce blocky approximations; the documented images are small and constrained by channel width. These are scoped technical limits, not proof of quality for a general image workflow.

### How it uses AI

A neural-art experiment that encodes trained tiny networks as ICC color-profile matrices and curves; compatible color engines turn gradient carrier images into paintings. [Source](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A neural-art experiment that encodes trained tiny networks as ICC color-profile matrices and curves; compatible color engines turn gradient carrier images into paintings. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Exploring file-format computation, colour pipelines and neural representations as interactive or educational artwork. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The linked browser page includes its own network evaluator. The author supplies carriers, expected outputs and an ImageMagick reproduction; the animated command illustration is explicitly not a screen recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

The simplest reproduction needs ImageMagick 6 or 7 and the repository carriers plus sRGB.icc. Training/rebuilding additionally needs Python 3.10+, NumPy, Pillow, PyTorch and LittleCMS 2. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The simplest reproduction needs ImageMagick 6 or 7 and the repository carriers plus sRGB.icc. Training/rebuilding additionally needs Python 3.10+, NumPy, Pillow, PyTorch and LittleCMS 2.

```sh
magick gallery/trio.png -profile sRGB.icc painted.png
```


```sh
magick compare -metric AE -fuzz 1% painted.png results/expected/trio.png null:
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

1. Download the source or published carrier bundle.
2. Convert a carrier through the supplied plain sRGB profile and compare with expected output.
3. Use the interactive page to inspect network elements and interpolate the painting code.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** ImageMagick 6/7 with LittleCMS for reproduction; optional Python 3.10+ stack for training. Browser demo uses a custom JavaScript evaluator.
- **Platforms:** Author measurements cover macOS and Ubuntu CI. Windows and Adobe colour engines are explicitly unknown; normal browser colour management does not render the neural painting.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** The software is MIT. The example paintings are identified as public-domain works with source attribution; no external model API is required.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The software is MIT. The example paintings are identified as public-domain works with source attribution; no external model API is required.
- **Cost:** Local compute; no paid API documented.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/fMindstormer/iccnet/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A speech-to-avatar pipeline combining Silero VAD, Whisper, an Ollama Cloud response, gTTS and VideoReTalking lip synchronization. [Source](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A speech-to-avatar pipeline combining Silero VAD, Whisper, an Ollama Cloud response, gTTS and VideoReTalking lip synchronization. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Producing a talking-head reply clip from microphone input and an authorized face-video asset. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links an output demonstration and example face/audio files. Despite the title, its documented output latency is about 2–5 minutes on a mid-range GPU, not continuous live lip synchronization. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Follow the Windows Conda/Python 3.8 route with FFmpeg, PyTorch CUDA 11.8 and the pinned compatibility packages. Download the separate checkpoint bundle and configure the voice-agent client without publishing credentials. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Follow the Windows Conda/Python 3.8 route with FFmpeg, PyTorch CUDA 11.8 and the pinned compatibility packages. Download the separate checkpoint bundle and configure the voice-agent client without publishing credentials.

```sh
python inference.py --face examples/face/women.mp4 --audio examples/audio/women_audio.wav --outfile results/output.mp4 --LNet_batch_size 4 --fast
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

1. First test an authorized face clip and short audio with inference-only mode.
2. Inspect alignment and artifacts; fast mode skips face enhancement.
3. For the voice-agent path configure the chosen cloud model, speak, and wait for the rendered reply clip.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/VedantR3907/local-ai-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Approximately 4 GB of checkpoints is documented. The README describes batch size 4 for 6–8 GB GPUs, not a universal VRAM minimum; CPU is very slow and minimum RAM is unspecified.
- **Software:** Windows Conda/Python 3.8, FFmpeg, CUDA 11.8+ recommended, Whisper/Silero/gTTS/Ollama and VideoReTalking dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Windows setup is documented; other platforms are not established in this fork.

### Install

Choose an official desktop release or use the browser edition. Source development uses npm install and npm run dev; desktop builds additionally need the Tauri toolchain. Configure either Anthropic access or a separate local model. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose an official desktop release or use the browser edition. Source development uses npm install and npm run dev; desktop builds additionally need the Tauri toolchain. Configure either Anthropic access or a separate local model.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a few primitives and ask the assistant for a constrained change.
2. Inspect dimensions, boolean operations and placement.
3. Export a mesh and validate geometry and fabrication constraints separately.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Documented local tiers are 3.3 GB download/8 GB memory, 6.6 GB/16 GB and 22 GB/32 GB. CPU inference is supported but slower; these are selected model tiers, not browser-editor minima.
- **Software:** Browser edition; desktop uses Tauri, Three.js and Manifold WASM. Ollama is optional; source Node version is not specified. Android development requires SDK/NDK.
- **Platforms:** Releases/browser routes are documented for desktop platforms and Android; iOS uses the browser. Browser and Android clients do not automatically install local models.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE) [Source 2](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** The root Apache-2.0 option was reviewed; the README offers MIT or Apache-2.0. Qwen/Ollama and Claude terms remain separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The root Apache-2.0 option was reviewed; the README offers MIT or Apache-2.0. Qwen/Ollama and Claude terms remain separate.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Installers lack paid developer signing certificates. Model outputs can be geometrically wrong, and mesh export does not certify printability. The web app cannot install Ollama; Android can use a separately hosted instance. [Source 1](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/GPTmadeit/formwright/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Transcription/rendering are local, but assistant requests send timeline, transcripts and selected frames to Anthropic. Optional remote access needs a password; it is not enabled by this report.

### How it uses AI

A local video editor with Whisper transcription and an AI assistant that edits an inspectable timeline through Claude Code or the Anthropic API. [Source](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local video editor with Whisper transcription and an AI assistant that edits an inspectable timeline through Claude Code or the Anthropic API. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Editing spoken-video projects, caption styles, speaker colours and layered effects while retaining project versions. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README describes the editor and assistant tools; mock/render checks and a complete guide do not verify autonomous editing quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Obtain the official macOS/Windows release or build from source with uv, Node 22+ and pnpm. Build the web interface, then start the Python engine; FFmpeg is downloaded automatically. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the official macOS/Windows release or build from source with uv, Node 22+ and pnpm. Build the web interface, then start the Python engine; FFmpeg is downloaded automatically.

```sh
cd engine && uv sync
```


```sh
cd web && pnpm install && pnpm build
```


```sh
cd engine && uv run python -m cadre_engine.run
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import media and create a local transcript.
2. Ask the assistant for a limited edit and inspect the resulting timeline.
3. Review the rendered preview, revise cuts/captions and export.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The optional voices module is described as about 2 GB. Minimum system RAM, GPU/VRAM and total project storage are not documented.
- **Software:** uv, Node 22+, pnpm for source; local Python engine and FFmpeg. Optional voices module is separate. Claude Code session or Anthropic API key for the AI assistant.
- **Platforms:** Desktop releases target Apple Silicon/Intel macOS and Windows 10/11. Optional browser remote access is not a separate native mobile editor.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app source; Whisper/voice models, Remotion components and Anthropic access have separate terms that must be checked for the chosen use.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT app source; Whisper/voice models, Remotion components and Anthropic access have separate terms that must be checked for the chosen use.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Transcription/rendering are local, but assistant requests send timeline, transcripts and selected frames to Anthropic. Optional remote access needs a password; it is not enabled by this report. [Source 1](https://github.com/kiy0ni/cadre/blob/main/README.md)

## TriplaneGaussian

3D, reconstruction & assets · Photogrammetry, scanning & neural rendering · Spatial audio & volumetric media

First full guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry. This is a researched baseline, not a claim of a new launch or a significant upgrade today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring fast viewable 3D reconstructions from a reference image and exporting Gaussian assets for compatible viewers.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-29. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The project links a Gradio demo and real/generated-image reconstruction examples. Its speed and quality claims are author-reported; this is an older research release newly profiled here. Open issue #34 reports blur and reconstruction fidelity below the published examples; #29 reports a hosted-demo runtime error, and #28 reports CUDA memory exhaustion on one roughly 10 GB device. These are specific user reports, not controlled benchmarks or a universal VRAM minimum. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.8+ and the documented PyTorch/CUDA route; compile pointnet2_ops and install pytorch_scatter, the Gaussian rasterizer, PyTorch3D and requirements. Download the reconstruction checkpoint; optional background removal also needs SAM. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** No release was returned in the recent-release snapshot. The bounded recent-activity review includes #34 open: How can I achieve the same level of reconstruction accuracy as the website?; #29 open: Gradio demo on HuggingFace Spaces gives error; #33 open: What resolution have the images in the training set?. Up to five releases and ten recently updated issue/PR records were inspected. This does not establish sustained support or resolve workflow-specific blockers. [Source](https://github.com/vast-ai-research/triplanegaussian/releases) [Source](https://github.com/vast-ai-research/triplanegaussian/issues)
- **Independent use · documented:** Independent users report running reconstruction, with unresolved quality, memory and hosted-demo problems. This documents use but does not verify a reliable creative workflow. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 project code; pretrained weights, SAM, source data and the separately installed Gaussian rasterizer retain their own terms. The complete runtime’s commercial-use permissions remain unverified. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/34) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/29) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/TriplaneGaussian/issues/28)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Trained on the approximately 45,000-model Objaverse-LVIS subset. PLY is a Gaussian representation, not a watertight printable mesh. Reported sub-second performance is not a universal hardware guarantee. Open issue #34 reports blur and reconstruction fidelity below the published examples; #29 reports a hosted-demo runtime error, and #28 reports CUDA memory exhaustion on one roughly 10 GB device. These are specific user reports, not controlled benchmarks or a universal VRAM minimum.

### How it uses AI

A pretrained transformer-based system reconstructing a hybrid triplane/Gaussian representation from a single image. ### Introduction

A pretrained transformer-based system reconstructing a hybrid triplane/Gaussian representation from a single image. - **Platforms:** Release 0.8.8 supplies Windows x64 and experimental Apple Silicon macOS 14+ packages. Mac checks were on virtual hardware, with software export rather than a verified VideoToolbox path. Intel Macs are unsupported. Linux Docker remains a separately documented 0.8.1 route.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Capture hiding behaves differently on older Windows versions. Whisper is a roadmap idea, not the current recognition backend. Unsigned application installers are distinct from signed updater artifacts.

### How it uses AI

A voice-following teleprompter uses offline Vosk recognition to track a spoken script and lets AI clients load or control scripts through MCP. [Source](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A voice-following teleprompter uses offline Vosk recognition to track a spoken script and lets AI clients load or control scripts through MCP. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Recording presentations or spoken videos at a natural pace with a script near the camera. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links voice-sync, resizing and colour demonstration clips. These are developer examples; recognition accuracy and capture exclusion are not independently validated. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the official Windows installer, select a microphone/language and download the speech model on first use. Source development requires Node 22, pnpm, Rust and Tauri prerequisites. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official Windows installer, select a microphone/language and download the speech model on first use. Source development requires Node 22, pnpm, Rust and Tauri prerequisites.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm tauri dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import or paste a script, using separators for slide cards.
2. Select language and microphone, rehearse and correct tracking by clicking a word.
3. Test capture exclusion in the actual recording app before recording; optionally connect an AI client for script authoring.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Each selected language model downloads approximately 30–90 MB. A microphone is required; RAM/VRAM and total disk minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Windows 10/11; capture hiding needs Windows 10 version 2004+. Source stack is Node 22/pnpm/Rust/Tauri with Vosk.
- **Platforms:** Windows desktop is documented; no macOS/Linux release is established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application; Vosk language models retain their own terms. Optional AI writing clients have separate service terms and may receive script text.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application; Vosk language models retain their own terms. Optional AI writing clients have separate service terms and may receive script text.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Capture hiding behaves differently on older Windows versions. Whisper is a roadmap idea, not the current recognition backend. Unsigned application installers are distinct from signed updater artifacts. [Source 1](https://github.com/FaureGalliard/vocal-teleprompter/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A local transcript editor runs Parakeet, Canary or Whisper in a browser or Rust CLI and turns word/time selections into video/audio edits. [Source](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local transcript editor runs Parakeet, Canary or Whisper in a browser or Rust CLI and turns word/time selections into video/audio edits. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Editing talks or interviews from text and exporting corrected subtitles, cut lists and shortened media. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The project links its browser app and editor screenshots. Documented frame-accurate editing and privacy claims are not independent performance tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the browser app and download its selected model, or use the documented Homebrew CLI package. Source development uses Bun plus the Rust/WASM toolchain. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the browser app and download its selected model, or use the documented Homebrew CLI package. Source development uses Bun plus the Rust/WASM toolchain.

```sh
brew install maxgfr/tap/verbatim
```


```sh
verbatim transcribe talk.mp4 -o talk.srt
```


```sh
verbatim cut talk.mp4 --delete 15:39-15:43 -o short.mp4 --dry-run
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import a recording and transcribe it locally.
2. Correct transcript text, select cuts and inspect the proposed edit plan.
3. Preview, apply and export the edited media plus subtitles or a reusable cut list.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The README describes about 670 MB for Parakeet and Whisper choices from about 80 MB. Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total working space are not documented.
- **Software:** Browser WebGPU/WebAssembly and WebCodecs paths; CLI uses ONNX Runtime and FFmpeg. Optional content-based instructions use configured cloud or local models.
- **Platforms:** Browser route depends on supported APIs; CLI releases target Apple Silicon macOS and Linux x64/arm64. Windows CLI and Intel macOS support are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app; Parakeet/Canary are declared CC BY 4.0, Whisper MIT and fonts OFL. Optional AI-provider terms are separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT app; Parakeet/Canary are declared CC BY 4.0, Whisper MIT and fonts OFL. Optional AI-provider terms are separate.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/maxgfr/verbatim/blob/main/README.md)

### Paper Unfold Mesh · MIT

Adding editable paper-reveal effects to code-rendered videos.

### MuseLab music workshop · MIT

Drafting editable musical phrases and exercises and exporting MIDI/MusicXML/ABC or audio.
An optional local/cloud LLM writes ABC notation that a strict parser turns into synchronized staff, numeric notation, piano-roll and mixer views; it can also draft lesson exercises.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Node 22.18+ is documented. AI providers have separate terms; fallback algorithmic composition is not model generation. Provider failures can fall back to deterministic music. The new 28-lesson curriculum evaluates screen-key input rather than recorded piano performance; progress stays in browser storage. Inspect the actual generation route and musical result.

### Rapid-H3 · Apache-2.0

Evaluating faster local H3 video drafts on a dedicated DGX Spark.
A ComfyUI node/preset package uses specialized INT8 kernels to accelerate MiniMax-H3 audiovisual generation on GB10.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Documented target: DGX Spark 128 GB, DGX OS/Docker, about 72 GB model storage plus about 21 GB image and optional 40 GB checkpoint. Speed/quality comparisons are author-specific experiments, not general guarantees. H3 weights and dependencies have separate terms.

### MOSS LoRA Convert · Apache-2.0

Moving an authorized image-style adapter between supported local inference stacks.
Converts MOSS-Image LoRA tensor layouts between ComfyUI/kohya/PEFT and mflux conventions so trained adapters can participate in generation.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Python 3.9+ and tensor dependencies are documented; no weights are included. Model/adapter licenses remain separate. Author round-trip tests do not establish support for every architecture or a general quality improvement.
